denotes the number of
f
flavors.

Appendix II
The topological heavy fermion model

The grand-canonical topological heavy fermion (THF) model for twisted bilayer graphene (TBG) is written as

H=H_{0}+H_{U_{1}}+H_{U_{2}}+H_{V}+H_{W}+H_{J}-\mu N

(S25)

where
N=\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}c^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}c_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}+\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}
is the total electron number operator.
The non-interacting part
H_{0}
is written as

\displaystyle H_{0}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\eta s}\sum_{aa^{\prime}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}}(H^{0,(c,\eta)}_{aa^{\prime}}({\mathbf{k}}+{\mathbf{G}}))c_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{{\mathbf{k}},a^{\prime}\eta s}+\sum_{\eta s\alpha s}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}}\bigg(H_{a\alpha}^{0,(cf,\eta)}({\mathbf{k}})c_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}+h.c.\bigg)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\eta s}\sum_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}}H^{0,(ff,\eta)}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}({\mathbf{k}}){f}^{\dagger}_{{{\mathbf{k}}},\alpha\eta s}{f}_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta s}

(S26)

where the matrix elements are

\displaystyle H^{0,(c,\eta)}({\mathbf{k}})=\begin{pmatrix}0_{2\times 2}&v_{\star}(\eta k_{x}\sigma_{0}+ik_{y}\sigma_{z})\\
v_{\star}(\eta k_{x}\sigma_{0}-ik_{y}\sigma_{z})&M\sigma_{x}\end{pmatrix}

(S27)

\displaystyle H^{0,(cf,\eta)}({\mathbf{k}})=\begin{pmatrix}\gamma\sigma_{0}+v_{\star}^{\prime}(\eta k_{x}\sigma_{x}+k_{y}\sigma_{y})\\
0_{2\times 2}\end{pmatrix}

(S28)

\displaystyle H^{0,(ff,\eta)}({\mathbf{k}})\approx 0_{2\times 2}

(S29)

The interaction terms are

\displaystyle H_{U_{1}}=

\displaystyle\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}:f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}::f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}:,

(S30)

\displaystyle H_{U_{2}}=

\displaystyle\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\sum_{\langle{\mathbf{R}},{\mathbf{R}}^{\prime}\rangle}:f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}::f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{{\mathbf{R}}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}:,

(S31)

\displaystyle H_{V}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\Omega_{0}N_{M}}\sum_{\eta sa}\sum_{\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}a^{\prime}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{q}}}V({\mathbf{q}}):c^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}c_{{\mathbf{k}}+{\mathbf{q}},a\eta s}::c^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}+{\mathbf{q}},a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}c_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}:,\;(|{\mathbf{k}}|,|{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}|,|{\mathbf{k}}+{\mathbf{q}}|,|{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}+{\mathbf{q}}|<\Lambda_{c}),

(S32)

\displaystyle H_{W}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\sum_{a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{q}}}W_{a^{\prime}}:f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}}+{\mathbf{q}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}::c^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}-{\mathbf{q}},a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}c_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}:,\;(|{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}|,|{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}-{\mathbf{q}}|<\Lambda_{c}),

(S33)

\displaystyle H_{J}=

\displaystyle-\frac{J}{2N_{M}}\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{q}}}[\eta\eta^{\prime}+(-1)^{\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}}]:f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}}+{\mathbf{q}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}::c^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}-{\mathbf{q}},\alpha^{\prime}+2\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}c_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},\alpha+2\eta s}:,\;(|{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}|,|{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}-{\mathbf{q}}|<\Lambda_{c}).

(S34)

where
N_{M}
is the number of moiré unit cell,
\Omega_{0}
is the unit cell size and

f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{M}}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}e^{-i{\mathbf{k}}\cdot{\mathbf{R}}}

(S35)

We also introduce the normal order notation, which is defined as

\displaystyle:c^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}c_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}:=

\displaystyle c^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}c_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{{\mathbf{k}}{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}\delta_{aa^{\prime}}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{ss^{\prime}}

(S36)

\displaystyle:f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}:=

\displaystyle f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{{\mathbf{k}}{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{ss^{\prime}}

(S37)

We define the relative filling number of each orbital as

\displaystyle\nu_{c,a}\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},\eta s}\bigg(\langle c^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}c_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\bigg)

(S38)

\displaystyle\nu_{f,\alpha}\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},\eta s}\bigg(\langle f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\bigg)

(S39)

In the symmetric phase with
C_{2z}T
symmetry, the filling numbers satisfy the constraints:
\nu_{c,1}=\nu_{c,2},\;\nu_{c,3}=\nu_{c,4}
and
\nu_{f,1}=\nu_{f,2}
.
In addition, the total relative filling numbers for
c
and
f
orbitals are defined as

\nu_{c}=\sum_{a=1}^{4}\nu_{c,a},\;\nu_{f}=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{2}\nu_{f,\alpha}.

(S40)

Treating
H_{U_{2}},H_{V},H_{W}
and
H_{J}
in the Hartree level, we obtain their contributions to the single particle Hamiltonian.

\displaystyle H_{U_{2}}^{\text{MF}}=

\displaystyle 6U_{2}\nu_{f}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}

(S41)

\displaystyle H_{V}^{\text{MF}}=

\displaystyle\frac{V(0)}{\Omega_{0}}\nu_{c}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}c^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}c_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}

(S42)

\displaystyle H_{W}^{\text{MF}}=

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{a^{\prime}}W_{a^{\prime}}\nu_{c,a^{\prime}}\bigg)\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}+\nu_{f}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}W_{a}c^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}c_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}

(S43)

\displaystyle H_{J}^{\text{MF}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{J}{4}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}\nu_{c,\alpha+2}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}-\frac{J}{4}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}\nu_{f,\alpha}c^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha+2\eta s}c_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha+2\eta s}

(S44)

For the
H_{U_{1}}
term, it is convenient to expand the normal ordering and rewrite it as

\displaystyle H_{U_{1}}=

\displaystyle\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}(f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}-1/2)(f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}-1/2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{N_{f}U_{1}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}+\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{(N_{f}-1)U_{1}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}+\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta s\neq\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}

(S45)

where
N_{f}=8
is the total number of
f
-electron flavors.
The effective Hamiltonian can then be written as

\displaystyle H^{\prime}=

\displaystyle H_{0}+\epsilon_{c,1}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},\eta s}\sum_{a=1,2}c^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}c_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}+\epsilon_{c,2}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},\eta s}\sum_{a=3,4}c^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}c_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}+\epsilon_{f}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}

\displaystyle+\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta s\neq\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}-\mu N

(S46)

where
\epsilon_{c,1}
,
\epsilon_{c,2}
, and
\epsilon_{f}
are the Hartree energy shifts arising from
H_{U_{2}}
,
H_{V}
,
H_{W}
,
H_{J}
, and the reordering of
H_{U_{1}}
. These quantities are defined as

\displaystyle\epsilon_{c,1}=

\displaystyle\frac{V(0)}{\Omega_{0}}\nu_{c}+W_{1}\nu_{f}

(S47)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{c,2}=

\displaystyle\frac{V(0)}{\Omega_{0}}\nu_{c}+W_{3}\nu_{f}-\frac{J}{8}\nu_{f}

(S48)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{f}=

\displaystyle 6U_{2}\nu_{f}+2W_{1}\nu_{c,1}+2W_{3}\nu_{c,3}-\frac{J}{4}\nu_{c,3}-\frac{(N_{f}-1)}{2}U_{1}

(S49)

Appendix III
Hybridization expansion

In this section, we evaluate the self-energy using the hybridization expansion method. We start from the mean-field Hamiltonian introduced in the previous section:

\displaystyle H^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\eta s}\sum_{aa^{\prime}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}}(H^{(c,\eta)}_{a,a^{\prime}}({\mathbf{k}}+{\mathbf{G}}))c_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{{\mathbf{k}},a^{\prime}\eta s}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{M}}}\sum_{\eta s,a\alpha}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}}\bigg(e^{-i{\mathbf{k}}\cdot{\mathbf{R}}}H_{a\alpha}^{(cf,\eta)}({\mathbf{k}})c_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}+h.c.\bigg)

\displaystyle+\sum_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}(\epsilon_{f}-\mu)f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}+{\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s\neq\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}}\,.

(S50)

where

\displaystyle H^{(c,\eta)}({\mathbf{k}})=\begin{pmatrix}\sigma_{0}(\epsilon_{c,1}-\mu)&v_{\star}(\eta k_{x}\sigma_{0}+ik_{y}\sigma_{z})\\
v_{\star}(\eta k_{x}\sigma_{0}-ik_{y}\sigma_{z})&\sigma_{0}(\epsilon_{c,2}-\mu)+M\sigma_{x}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle H^{(cf,\eta)}({\mathbf{k}})=\begin{pmatrix}\gamma\sigma_{0}+v_{\star}^{\prime}(\eta k_{x}\sigma_{x}+k_{y}\sigma_{y})\\
0_{2\times 2}\end{pmatrix}

(S51)

Here,
\epsilon_{f}
,
\epsilon_{c,1}
and
\epsilon_{c,2}
characterize the Hartree-Fock contributions from
H_{V}
,
H_{W}
,
H_{J}
and
H_{U_{2}}
as well as the single-particle contribution obtained by expanding the normal ordering of
H_{U_{1}}
81
.
Their specific definitions were given by
eq.

S49
.

f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}
and
c_{{\mathbf{k}},a\eta s}
denote the annihilation operators for
f
and
c
electrons, respectively.
We then integrate out the
c
electrons, which leads to the following action involving only the
f
electrons (see also Ref.
81
)

\displaystyle S=

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime},i\omega}f_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(i\omega)\bigg[(-i\omega-\mu+\epsilon_{f})\delta_{\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}+\gamma^{2}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}}^{\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}(i\omega)\bigg]f_{{\mathbf{k}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}(i\omega)

\displaystyle+{\int_{\tau}\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s\neq\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha\eta s}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}(\tau)}

(S52)

The hybridization function is defined as

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}^{\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}(i\omega)=\delta_{\eta s,\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\sum_{n,a_{1}a_{2}}[H^{(fc,\eta)}({\mathbf{k}})]_{\alpha a_{1}}U^{\eta,c}_{{\mathbf{k}},a_{1}n}\frac{1}{i\omega-E^{\eta,c}_{{\mathbf{k}},n}}U^{\eta,c,*}_{{\mathbf{k}},a_{2}n}[H^{(fc,\eta)}({\mathbf{k}})]^{*}_{\alpha^{\prime}a_{2}}

(S53)

where we have introduced the eigenvalues and eigenvectors of
H^{(c,\eta)}

\displaystyle\sum_{a^{\prime}}H^{(c,\eta)}_{aa^{\prime}}U_{{\mathbf{k}},a^{\prime}n}^{\eta,c}=E_{{\mathbf{k}},n}^{\eta,c}U_{{\mathbf{k}},an}^{\eta,c}\,.

(S54)

Note that we have factored out an overall prefactor
\gamma^{2}
in order to facilitate the perturbative expansion. Setting
\gamma^{2}=0
therefore indicates that all
fc
hybridization is turned off.

We first discuss symmetry constraints on the hybridization function. In the
f
-orbital subspace, the corresponding symmetry representations are

D^{f}(C_{3z})=e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}\sigma_{z}\tau_{z}},\quad D^{f}(C_{2x})=\sigma_{x}\tau_{0},\quad D^{f}(C_{2z})=\sigma_{x}\tau_{x}

(S55)

where
\sigma
and
\tau
are Pauli matrices acting on the orbital and valley degrees of freedom, respectively.
As will be shown below, only the local hybridization function enters our calculation. It is defined as

\Delta^{\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}(i\omega)\equiv\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}}\Delta^{\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}_{{\mathbf{k}}}(i\omega)

(S56)

Due to the spin
\mathrm{SU}(2)
and valley
\mathrm{U}_{V}(1)
symmetry, the hybridization function is diagonal in valley space and independent of spin (
eq.

S53
).
Thus, we have

\Delta^{\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}(i\omega)=\delta_{\eta s,\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\Delta_{\eta}^{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(i\omega)

(S57)

In orbital space,
\Delta_{\eta}^{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}
can be expanded in the Pauli-matrix basis as

\Delta_{\eta}^{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}=\sum_{i}\Delta_{\eta}^{i}\sigma_{i}^{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}
.

C_{3z}
symmetry requires that
\Delta_{\eta}^{i=x,y}=0
and
C_{2x}
symmetry requires that
\Delta_{\eta}^{i=y,z}=0
.
Thus, the only nonzero component is
\Delta_{\eta}^{i=0}
, which implies
\Delta_{\eta}^{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}=\delta_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\Delta_{\eta}
.
Using in addition the
C_{2z}
symmetry, which swaps the two valleys, we find that
\Delta_{\eta=+}=\Delta_{\eta=-}=\Delta
.
In summary, in the presence of spin
\mathrm{SU}(2)
, valley
\mathrm{U}_{V}(1)
,
C_{3z}
,
C_{2x}
and
C_{2z}
symmetries, the local hybridization function takes the form

\Delta^{\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}(i\omega)=\delta_{\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\Delta(i\omega)

(S58)

which exhibits a flavor
\mathrm{U}(8)
symmetry.

To simplify the notation, we use single indices
i
and
j
to collectively label the orbital
\alpha
, valley
\eta
, and spin
\sigma
degrees of freedom of the
f
electrons.
Then the action can be written as

\displaystyle S=

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},ij,i\omega}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}^{\dagger}(i\omega)[(-i\omega-\mu{+\epsilon_{f}})\delta_{i,j}+\gamma^{2}\Delta^{i,j}_{{\mathbf{k}}}(i\omega)]f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}(i\omega){+\frac{U_{1}}{2}\int_{\tau}\sum_{i\neq j,{\mathbf{R}}}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}^{\dagger}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}(\tau)}

(S59)

Since
H^{\prime}
(
appendix

III
) is quadratic in the
c
electron operators, integrating out the
c
electron degree of freedom to obtain the action
S
(
eq.

S59
) is exact.

We define the atomic Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle H_{atom}=

\displaystyle(\epsilon_{f}-\mu)\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},i}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},i}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}+\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{i\neq j}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},j}f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\epsilon_{f}-\mu)\sum_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}+\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{i\neq j}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},j}f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}

(S60)

where we have used the relation between
f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},i}
and
f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}
in
eq.

S35
.
If we treat
H_{U_{1}}
at the Hartree-Fock level, then the mean-field atomic Hamiltonian and action can be written as

\displaystyle H_{atom}^{\mathrm{MF}}=

\displaystyle\bigg[\epsilon_{f}-\mu+\frac{(N_{f}-1)n_{f}}{N_{f}}U_{1}\bigg]\sum_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}=\bigg[\epsilon_{f}-\mu+\frac{(N_{f}-1)n_{f}}{N_{f}}U_{1}\bigg]\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},i}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},i}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}

\displaystyle S^{\text{MF}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},ij,i\omega}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(i\omega)\bigg[\bigg(-i\omega-\mu+\epsilon_{f}+\frac{(N_{f}-1)n_{f}}{N_{f}}U_{1}\bigg)\delta_{i,j}+\gamma^{2}\Delta^{i,j}_{{\mathbf{k}}}(i\omega)\bigg]f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}(i\omega)

(S61)

where
n_{f}=\nu_{f}+N_{f}/2
(see definition of
\nu_{f}
in
eqs.

S40
and
S39
).
Thus, the interacting Green’s function at the Hartree-Fock level is

\displaystyle[G_{f}^{{\text{MF}}}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})]^{-1}_{ij}=[i\omega+\mu-\epsilon_{f}-\frac{(N_{f}-1){n_{f}}U_{1}}{N_{f}}]\delta_{i,j}-\gamma^{2}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}}^{i,j}(i\omega)

(S62)

where
G^{\text{MF}}_{f}
includes the Hartree-Fock contributions from
H_{U_{2}},H_{V},H_{W},H_{J}
(see definition of
\epsilon_{f}
in
eq.

S49
) and
H_{U_{1}}
in the symmetric state.

We are particularly interested in the Green’s function and the dynamical self-energy of the
f
electrons, defined as

G_{f,ij}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})=-\int_{\tau}\langle f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}^{\dagger}(0)\rangle e^{i\omega\tau}

(S63)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{f,ij}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})=[G^{{\text{MF}}}_{f}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})]^{-1}_{ij}-[G_{f}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})]^{-1}_{ij}

(S64)

To achieve this, we solve the model defined by the action
S
(
eq.

S59
) using a hybridization expansion, treating
\gamma^{2}
as a small parameter.
We first compute the Green’s function
G_{f,ij}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})
perturbatively to order
\gamma^{2}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}}^{i,j}
. We then use the relation between the Green’s function and the self-energy at order
\gamma^{2}
(from
eq.

S64
) to extract the self-energy to the same order.

Expanding in powers of
\gamma^{2}
, we write the Green’s function and self-energy as

\displaystyle G_{f,ij}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})=G^{(0)}_{f,ij}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})+G^{(1)}_{f,ij}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})+\mathcal{O}(\gamma^{4})

\displaystyle\Sigma_{f,ij}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})=\Sigma^{(0)}_{f,ij}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})+\Sigma^{(1)}_{f,ij}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})+\mathcal{O}(\gamma^{4})

(S65)

where
G_{f}^{(n)}
denotes the interacting Green’s function at order
\gamma^{2n}
, and
\Sigma_{f}^{(n)}
denotes the self-energy at order
\gamma^{2n}
.
Form the Dyson equation
eq.

S64
, we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{f,ij}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})=

\displaystyle[G^{{\text{MF}}}_{f}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})]^{-1}_{ij}-[G_{f}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})]^{-1}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[G^{{\text{MF}}}_{f}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})]^{-1}_{ij}-\bigg[G_{f}^{(0)}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})+G_{f}^{(1)}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})\bigg]_{ij}^{-1}+\mathcal{O}(\gamma^{4})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[G^{{\text{MF}}}_{f}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})]^{-1}_{ij}-\bigg[G_{f}^{(0)}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})\bigg]^{-1}_{ij}+\bigg[[G_{f}^{(0)}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})]^{-1}G_{f}^{(1)}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})[G_{f}^{(0)}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})]^{-1}\bigg]_{ij}+\mathcal{O}(\gamma^{4})

(S66)

Combining
eqs.

S63
,
S65
and
S66
, we find

\displaystyle\Sigma_{f,ij}^{(0)}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})=[i\omega+\mu-\epsilon_{f}-\frac{(N_{f}-1)n_{f}U_{1}}{N_{f}}]\delta_{i,j}-[G_{f}^{(0)}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})]^{-1}_{ij}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{f,ij}^{(1)}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})=-\gamma^{2}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}}^{ij}(i\omega)+\bigg[[G_{f}^{(0)}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})]^{-1}G_{f}^{(1)}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})[G_{f}^{(0)}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})]^{-1}\bigg]_{ij}

(S67)

Accordingly, by evaluating the Green’s function to order
\gamma^{2}
, we can also obtain the self-energy up to the same order through
eq.

S67
.

We note that, at zeroth order in
\gamma
,
G_{f}^{(0)}
corresponds to the Green’s function in the
\gamma=0
limit, namely, the atomic Green’s function. The associated self-energy
\Sigma_{f}^{(0)}
then reduces to the Hubbard-I self-energy evaluated in Ref.
81
.

III.1
Formulas for
G_{f}^{(1)}
and
\Sigma_{f}^{(1)}

We now derive the
\mathcal{O}(\gamma^{2})
contribution to the self-energy,
\Sigma_{f,ij}^{(1)}
, by calculating
G_{f}^{(1)}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})
.
To carry out the perturbative calculation, we first separate the action into two parts:

\displaystyle S=S_{atom}+\gamma^{2}S_{hyb}

\displaystyle S_{atom}=\sum_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}^{\dagger}(-i\omega-\mu)f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}+\frac{U_{1}}{2}\int_{\tau}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}[n_{f,{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau)-n_{0}]^{2}

\displaystyle S_{hyb}=\sum_{{\mathbf{k}},ij,\omega}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}^{\dagger}(i\omega)\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}}^{ij}(i\omega)f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}(i\omega)

(S68)

where
{n}_{f,{\mathbf{R}}}=\sum_{m}f_{{\mathbf{R}},m}^{\dagger}f_{{\mathbf{R}},m}
and
n_{0}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\epsilon_{f}}{U_{1}}
.

In
section

III.1
we have rewritten the atomic Hamiltonian defined in
eq.

S60
as

\displaystyle H_{atom}=

\displaystyle(\epsilon_{f}-\mu)\sum_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}+\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{i\neq j}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},j}f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(\epsilon_{f}-\frac{U_{1}}{2}\bigg)\sum_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}+\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},j}f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}-\mu\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}n_{f,{\mathbf{R}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}\bigg[2\bigg(\frac{\epsilon_{f}}{U_{1}}-\frac{1}{2}\bigg){n}_{f,{\mathbf{R}}}+{n}_{f,{\mathbf{R}}}^{2}\bigg]-\mu\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}n_{f,{\mathbf{R}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}(n_{f,{\mathbf{R}}}-n_{0})^{2}-\mu\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}n_{f,{\mathbf{R}}}+\text{const.}

(S69)

Then, up to order
\gamma^{2}
, the partition function is

\displaystyle Z=

\displaystyle\int D[f,f^{\dagger}]{e^{-S}}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\int D[f,f^{\dagger}](1-\gamma^{2}S_{hyb})e^{-S_{atom}}+\mathcal{O}(\gamma^{4})

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle Z_{\gamma=0}-\gamma^{2}Z_{\gamma=0}\sum_{ij,{\mathbf{k}},i\omega}\Delta^{ij}_{\mathbf{k}}(i\omega)\langle f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}^{\dagger}(i\omega)f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}(i\omega)\rangle_{\gamma=0}+\mathcal{O}(\gamma^{4})

(S70)

where
Z_{\gamma=0}
denotes the partition function in the
\gamma=0
limit,

\displaystyle Z_{\gamma=0}=\int D[f,f^{\dagger}]e^{-S_{atom}}

(S71)

The expectation value of an operator
O
in the
\gamma=0
limit is defined as

\displaystyle\langle O\rangle_{\gamma=0}=\frac{1}{Z_{\gamma=0}}\int D[f,f^{\dagger}]Oe^{-S_{atom}}

(S72)

We note that, in order to evaluate the partition function
Z
, we need to calculate the Green’s function of the
f
electrons in the
\gamma=0
limit (
section

III.1
), namely, the atomic Green’s function defined as

\displaystyle G_{f,ij}^{(0)}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})=-\langle f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}(i\omega)f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}^{\dagger}(i\omega)\rangle_{\gamma=0}=-\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}^{\dagger}(0)\rangle_{\gamma=0}e^{i\omega\tau}

(S73)

Since
f
electrons on different sites
{\mathbf{R}}
are decoupled in the atomic limit (
\gamma=0
), the atomic Green’s function is independent of
{\mathbf{k}}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle G_{f,ij}^{(0)}(\tau,{\mathbf{k}})=-\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(\tau)f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},j}(0)\rangle_{\gamma=0}

(S74)

with
N_{M}
the number of moiré unit cells.
In the absence of strain, which is the case considered here, the atomic limit has an enlarged
\mathrm{U}(8)
symmetry
81
. This implies that the atomic Green’s function is diagonal in the flavor basis:

\displaystyle G_{f,ij}^{(0)}(\tau,{\mathbf{k}})\propto\delta_{i,j}

(S75)

Moreover, by translation symmetry, the expectation value
\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(\tau)f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},j}(0)\rangle_{\gamma=0}
is independent of
{\mathbf{R}}
, and we can therefore introduce the local Green’s function as

\displaystyle G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})=-\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})\rangle_{\gamma=0}

(S76)

such that

\displaystyle G_{f,ij}^{(0)}(\tau,{\mathbf{k}})=\delta_{i,j}G_{f,loc}(\tau)

(S77)

We define the Fourier transformation of the local Green’s function as

\displaystyle G_{f,loc}(i\omega)=-\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}^{\dagger}(0)\rangle_{\gamma=0}e^{i\omega\tau}

(S78)

Since this is a fermionic system,
G_{f,\mathrm{loc}}(\tau)
satisfies

G_{f,loc}(\tau+\beta)=-G_{f,loc}(\tau)\implies G_{f,loc}(\tau)=\frac{1}{\beta}\sum_{i\omega}G_{f,loc}(i\omega)e^{-i\omega\tau}

(S79)

where
i\omega
is the Matsubara frequency for Fermion.
The Fourier transformation could be calculated as

G_{f,loc}(i\omega)=\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\;G_{f,loc}(\tau)e^{i\omega\tau}=\int_{-\beta}^{0}G_{f,loc}(\tau+\beta)e^{i\omega(\tau+\beta)}=\int_{-\beta}^{0}d\tau\;G_{f,loc}(\tau)e^{i\omega\tau}

(S80)

In the last equality, we used the fact that
e^{i\omega\beta}=-1
for fermionic Matsubara frequencies.
The formula with integration over
[0,\beta]
gives

\displaystyle G_{f,loc}(i\omega)=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\;\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(\tau>0)f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(0)\rangle_{\gamma=0}e^{i\omega\tau}=-\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\;\langle f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(\tau)f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(0)\rangle_{\gamma=0}e^{i\omega\tau}=-\langle f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(i\omega)f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(i\omega)\rangle_{\gamma=0}

(S81)

while the formula with integration over
[-\beta,0]
gives

\displaystyle G_{f,loc}(i\omega)=

\displaystyle-\int_{-\beta}^{0}d\tau\;\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(\tau<0)f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(0)\rangle_{\gamma=0}e^{i\omega\tau}=\int_{-\beta}^{0}d\tau\;\langle f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(0)f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(\tau)\rangle_{\gamma=0}e^{i\omega\tau}=\langle f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(i\omega)f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(i\omega)\rangle_{\gamma=0}

(S82)

We have used the definition of
f(i\omega)
as

f(i\omega)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\;f(\tau)e^{i\omega\tau}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta}}\int_{-\beta}^{0}d\tau\;f(\tau+\beta)e^{i\omega(\tau+\beta)}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta}}\int_{-\beta}^{0}d\tau\;f(\tau)e^{i\omega\tau}

(S83)

In summary,

G_{f,loc}(i\omega)=-\langle f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(i\omega)f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(i\omega)\rangle_{\gamma=0}=\langle f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(i\omega)f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(i\omega)\rangle_{\gamma=0}

(S84)

Another way to understand this result is that in
section

III.1
,
f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(i\omega)
and
f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}(i\omega)
are not operators, but Grassmann numbers in the path integral language.
They therefore anticommute, which gives
\langle f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(i\omega)f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}(i\omega)\rangle_{\gamma=0}=-\langle f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}(i\omega)f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(i\omega)\rangle_{\gamma=0}

Using
eqs.

S77
and
S84
, the partition function can be written as (
section

III.1
)

\displaystyle Z\approx Z_{\gamma=0}\bigg[1-\gamma^{2}\sum_{j,{\mathbf{k}},i\omega}\Delta^{jj}_{{\mathbf{k}}}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega)\bigg]+\mathcal{O}(\gamma^{4})

(S85)

In
eq.

S67
, we need the first-order correction in
\gamma^{2}
to the
f
-electron Green’s function, namely
G_{f}^{(1)}
.
This requires us to evaluate the Green’s function in the presence of the
cf
hybridization, defined as

G_{f,ij}(\tau-\tau^{\prime},{\mathbf{k}})\equiv-\frac{1}{Z}\int D[f,f^{\dagger}]T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})e^{-S}

(S86)

We now evaluate the corresponding path integral using hybridization expansion:

\displaystyle-\int D[f,f^{\dagger}]T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime}){e^{-S}}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle-\int D[f,f^{\dagger}]T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})e^{-S_{atom}}

\displaystyle+\gamma^{2}\int D[f,f^{\dagger}]T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},i^{\prime}j^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime}}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(i\omega^{\prime})f_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},i^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(i\omega^{\prime})f_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},j^{\prime}}(i\omega^{\prime})e^{-S_{atom}}+\mathcal{O}(\gamma^{4})

(S87)

We notice that

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}\omega^{\prime},i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(i\omega^{\prime})f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},i^{\prime}}(i\omega^{\prime})f_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},j^{\prime}}(i\omega^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},i^{\prime}j^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(\tau_{3})f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},i^{\prime}}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},j^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})e^{i\omega^{\prime}(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}+\tau_{3})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})f_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},i^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},j^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{R}}_{3},{\mathbf{R}}_{4}}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{3},i^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{4},j^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})e^{-i{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}\cdot({\mathbf{R}}_{4}-{\mathbf{R}}_{3})}

(S88)

where we used the abbreviation
\int_{\tau}=\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau
for simplicity.
In the following text, we will always use
\int_{\tau}
for the integration over interval
[0,\beta]
. For other intervals, the integration will be written out explicitly.

We have also used the following Fourier transforms:

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}^{ij}(\tau)=\frac{1}{\beta}\sum_{i\omega}\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}^{ij}(i\omega)e^{-i\omega\tau},\quad f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(i\omega)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta}}\int_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(\tau)e^{i\omega\tau}

(S89)

Then we find

\displaystyle-\int D[f,f^{\dagger}]T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime}){e^{-S}}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle-\int D[f,f^{\dagger}]T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})e^{-S_{atom}}

\displaystyle+\gamma^{2}\int D[f,f^{\dagger}]T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{R}}_{3},{\mathbf{R}}_{4}}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{3},i^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{4},j^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})e^{-i{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}\cdot({\mathbf{R}}_{4}-{\mathbf{R}}_{3})}e^{-S_{atom}}+\mathcal{O}(\gamma^{4})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Z_{\gamma=0}\bigg[\delta_{i,j}G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})+\gamma^{2}\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\frac{1}{N_{M}^{2}}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{R}}_{3},{\mathbf{R}}_{4}}\sum_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime},{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})e^{-i{\mathbf{k}}\cdot({\mathbf{R}}_{1}-{\mathbf{R}}_{2})-i{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}({\mathbf{R}}_{4}-{\mathbf{R}}_{3})}\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{2},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{3},i^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{4},j^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{\gamma=0}\bigg]+\mathcal{O}(\gamma^{4})

(S90)

We now aim to evaluate

\displaystyle\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{2},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{3},i^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{4},j^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{\gamma=0}

(S91)

Since the action at
\gamma=0
describes an atomic Hamiltonian with decoupled sites, the non-zero contribution can arise only from the following situations, where the number of created and annihilated
f
electrons is the same on each site:

•

{\mathbf{R}}_{1}={\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}={\mathbf{R}}_{4},{\mathbf{R}}_{1}\neq{\mathbf{R}}_{3}
.

•

{\mathbf{R}}_{1}={\mathbf{R}}_{3},{\mathbf{R}}_{4}={\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{R}}_{1}\neq{\mathbf{R}}_{4}
.

•

{\mathbf{R}}_{1}={\mathbf{R}}_{2}={\mathbf{R}}_{3}={\mathbf{R}}_{4}
.

Because this is an atomic problem, the
f
electrons on different sites are decoupled. Therefore, the first two cases are easily evaluated:

•

{\mathbf{R}}_{1}={\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}={\mathbf{R}}_{4},{\mathbf{R}}_{1}\neq{\mathbf{R}}_{3}
:

\displaystyle\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{3},i^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{3},j^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{\gamma=0}=\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})\rangle_{\gamma=0}\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{3},i^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{3},j^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{\gamma=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\delta_{i,j}\delta_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})

(S92)

•

{\mathbf{R}}_{1}={\mathbf{R}}_{3},{\mathbf{R}}_{2}={\mathbf{R}}_{4},{\mathbf{R}}_{1}\neq{\mathbf{R}}_{4}
:

\displaystyle\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{4},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},i^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{4},j^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{\gamma=0}=-\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},i^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})\rangle_{\gamma=0}\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{4},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{4},j^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{\gamma=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{i,i^{\prime}}\delta_{j,j^{\prime}}G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau_{1})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau^{\prime})

(S93)

Using the results of the first two cases, we rewrite the four-point correlation function as

\displaystyle\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{2},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{3},i^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}}_{4},j^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{\gamma=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{2}}\delta_{{\mathbf{R}}_{3},{\mathbf{R}}_{4}}(1-\delta_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}})\delta_{i,j}\delta_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}(-1)G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})

\displaystyle+{\delta_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}}\delta_{{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{R}}_{4}}(1-\delta_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{4}})\delta_{i,i^{\prime}}\delta_{j,j^{\prime}}G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau_{1})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau^{\prime})}

\displaystyle+\delta_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{2}}\delta_{{\mathbf{R}}_{3},{\mathbf{R}}_{4}}\delta_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}}\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})f_{{\mathbf{R}},i^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}},j^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{\gamma=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{2}}\delta_{{\mathbf{R}}_{3},{\mathbf{R}}_{4}}\delta_{i,j}\delta_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}(-1)G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})+{\delta_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}}\delta_{{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{R}}_{4}}\delta_{i,i^{\prime}}\delta_{j,j^{\prime}}G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau_{1})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau^{\prime})}

\displaystyle+\delta_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{2}}\delta_{{\mathbf{R}}_{3},{\mathbf{R}}_{4}}\delta_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}}\tilde{F}^{iji^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})

(S94)

where
\tilde{F}^{iji^{\prime}j^{\prime}}
is defined as

\displaystyle\tilde{F}^{iji^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\equiv

\displaystyle\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})f_{{\mathbf{R}},i^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}},j^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{\gamma=0}

\displaystyle+\delta_{i,j}\delta_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})-{\delta_{i,i^{\prime}}\delta_{j,j^{\prime}}G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau_{1})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau^{\prime})}

(S95)

From
eq.

S94
, we see that the four-point correlation function is decomposed into two parts: one involving products of two Green’s functions, and the other encoded in
\tilde{F}^{iji^{\prime}j^{\prime}}
, which represents the remaining local contribution.
Combining
eqs.

S90
and
S94
, we obtain

\displaystyle-\int D[f,f^{\dagger}]T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime}){e^{-S}}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle Z_{\gamma=0}\bigg\{G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})\delta_{i,j}+\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\gamma^{2}\Delta^{i^{\prime}i^{\prime}}_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})\bigg[-\delta_{i,j}G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})\bigg]

\displaystyle+\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\gamma^{2}\Delta^{ij}_{{\mathbf{k}}}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau_{1})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau^{\prime})+\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\gamma^{2}\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})\tilde{F}^{iji^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\bigg\}

(S96)

From
eq.

S58
,
we note that the local hybridization function,
\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}^{ij}
, also respects the flavor U
(8)
symmetry (also see Ref.
154
).
We can introduce the symmetric local hybridization function
\Delta(\tau)
through

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}}^{ij}(\tau)=\delta_{i,j}\Delta(\tau)

(S97)

We therefore have

\displaystyle-\int D[f,f^{\dagger}]T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime}){e^{-S}}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle Z_{\gamma=0}\bigg\{G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})\delta_{i,j}+\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\gamma^{2}\Delta^{i^{\prime}i^{\prime}}_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})\bigg[-\delta_{i,j}G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})\bigg]

\displaystyle+\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\gamma^{2}\Delta^{ij}_{{\mathbf{k}}}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau_{1})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau^{\prime})+\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}}\gamma^{2}\Delta(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})\tilde{F}^{iji^{\prime}i^{\prime}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Z_{\gamma=0}\bigg\{G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})\delta_{i,j}+\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\gamma^{2}\Delta^{i^{\prime}i^{\prime}}_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})\bigg[-\delta_{i,j}G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})\bigg]

\displaystyle+\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\gamma^{2}\Delta^{ij}_{{\mathbf{k}}}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau_{1})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau^{\prime})+\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}}\gamma^{2}\Delta(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})\tilde{F}^{iii^{\prime}i^{\prime}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\delta_{i,j}\bigg\}

(S98)

where we have used the fact that
F^{iji^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\propto\delta_{i,j}
. The argument is as follows.
Since the atomic Hamiltonian
eq.

S69
has flavor U
(8)
symmetry, the flavor pairs
(ij^{\prime})
and
(ji^{\prime})
must be the same in the correlation function
\tilde{F}^{iji^{\prime}j^{\prime}}
.
Therefore, it takes the form

\tilde{F}^{iji^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\propto\delta_{i,j}\delta_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}f_{1}+\delta_{i,i^{\prime}}\delta_{j^{\prime},j}f_{2}

(S99)

which implies

\tilde{F}^{iji^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\propto\delta_{i,j}f_{1}+\delta_{i,i^{\prime}}\delta_{i^{\prime},j}f_{2}\propto\delta_{i,j}

(S100)

To simplify the notation, we define

\displaystyle F^{ij}(\tau,\tau^{\prime},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=\tilde{F}^{iijj}(\tau,\tau^{\prime},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})

\displaystyle F^{ij}(\tau,\tau^{\prime},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=F^{ij}(\tau-\tau^{\prime},0,\tau_{1}-\tau^{\prime},\tau_{2}-\tau^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}\sum_{i\omega_{1},i\omega_{2},i\omega}F^{ij}(i\omega,i\omega_{1},i\omega_{2})e^{-i\omega(\tau-\tau^{\prime})+i\omega_{1}(\tau_{1}-\tau^{\prime})-i\omega_{2}(\tau_{2}-\tau^{\prime})}

(S101)

More explicitly, using
section

III.1
,
F^{ij}
is given by

\displaystyle F^{ij}(\tau,\tau^{\prime},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=

\displaystyle\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{\gamma=0}

\displaystyle+G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})-{\delta_{i,j}G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau_{1})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau^{\prime})}

(S102)

Expressing
\tilde{F}^{iijj}
via
F^{ij}
, we find

\displaystyle-\int D[f,f^{\dagger}]T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime}){e^{-S}}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle Z_{\gamma=0}\bigg\{G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})\delta_{i,j}+\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\gamma^{2}\Delta^{i^{\prime}i^{\prime}}_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})\bigg[-\delta_{i,j}G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})\bigg]

\displaystyle+\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\gamma^{2}\Delta^{ij}_{{\mathbf{k}}}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})G_{f,loc}(\tau-\tau_{1})G_{f,loc}(\tau_{2}-\tau^{\prime})+\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}}\gamma^{2}\Delta(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})F^{ii^{\prime}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\delta_{i,j}\bigg\}

(S103)

This expression can also be transformed to Matsubara frequency space as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{\tau,\tau^{\prime}}\bigg[-\int D[f,f^{\dagger}]T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{k}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime}){e^{-S}}\bigg]e^{i\omega(\tau-\tau^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle Z_{\gamma=0}\bigg[G_{f,loc}(i\omega)\delta_{i,j}

\displaystyle-\delta_{i,j}\frac{1}{\beta^{4}}\sum_{i\omega^{\prime},i\omega_{1},i\omega_{2}}\gamma^{2}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}^{i^{\prime}i^{\prime}}(i\omega^{\prime})G_{f,loc}(i\omega_{1})G_{f,loc}(i\omega_{2})\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau,\tau^{\prime}}e^{-i\omega^{\prime}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})-i\omega_{1}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})-i\omega_{2}(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})+i\omega(\tau-\tau^{\prime})}

\displaystyle+\gamma^{2}\frac{1}{\beta^{4}}\sum_{i\omega^{\prime},i\omega_{1},i\omega_{2}}\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}^{ij}(i\omega^{\prime})G_{f,loc}(i\omega_{1})G_{f,loc}(i\omega_{2})\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau,\tau^{\prime}}e^{-i\omega^{\prime}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})-i\omega_{1}(\tau-\tau_{1})-i\omega_{2}(\tau_{2}-\tau^{\prime})+i\omega(\tau-\tau^{\prime})}

\displaystyle+\delta_{i,j}\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\beta^{4}}\sum_{i\omega^{\prime},i\omega_{1},i\omega_{2},i\omega_{3}}\sum_{i^{\prime}}\Delta(i\omega^{\prime})F^{ii^{\prime}}(i\omega_{3},i\omega_{1},i\omega_{2})\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau,\tau^{\prime}}e^{-i\omega^{\prime}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})-i\omega_{3}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})+i\omega_{1}(\tau_{1}-\tau^{\prime})-i\omega_{2}(\tau_{2}-\tau^{\prime})+i\omega(\tau-\tau^{\prime})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Z_{\gamma=0}\bigg[G_{f,loc}(i\omega)\delta_{i,j}-\delta_{i,j}\gamma^{2}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},i^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime}}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}^{i^{\prime}i^{\prime}}(i\omega^{\prime})G_{f,loc}(i\omega^{\prime})G_{f,loc}(i\omega)

\displaystyle+\gamma^{2}\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}^{i,j}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega)+\gamma^{2}\delta_{i,j}\sum_{i\omega^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\Delta(i\omega^{\prime})F^{ii^{\prime}}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime})\bigg]

(S104)

Therefore, within hybridization expansion, the Green’s function reads (combining
eqs.

S85
and
III.1
)

\displaystyle G_{f,ij}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Z}\int_{\tau}-\langle f_{{\mathbf{k}},i}(\tau)f^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{k}},j}(0)\rangle e^{i\omega\tau}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\bigg[1-\gamma^{2}\sum_{j,{\mathbf{k}},i\omega}\Delta^{jj}_{{\mathbf{k}}}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega)\bigg]^{-1}\times\bigg[G_{f,loc}(i\omega)\delta_{i,j}-\delta_{i,j}\gamma^{2}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime},i^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime}}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}}^{i^{\prime}i^{\prime}}(i\omega^{\prime})G_{f,loc}(i\omega^{\prime})G_{f,loc}(i\omega)

\displaystyle+\gamma^{2}\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}^{ij}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega)+\gamma^{2}\delta_{i,j}\sum_{i\omega^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\Delta(i\omega^{\prime})F^{ii^{\prime}}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime})\bigg]

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\delta_{i,j}G_{f,loc}(i\omega)+\gamma^{2}\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}^{ij}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega)+\gamma^{2}\delta_{i,j}\sum_{i\omega^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\Delta(i\omega^{\prime})F^{ii^{\prime}}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime})+\mathcal{O}(\gamma^{4})

(S105)

By comparing the above equation with the first equation in
eq.

S65
, we obtain

\displaystyle G^{(0)}_{f,ij}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})=\delta_{i,j}G_{f,loc}(i\omega)

\displaystyle G^{(1)}_{f,ij}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})=\gamma^{2}\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}^{ij}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega)+\gamma^{2}\delta_{i,j}\sum_{i\omega^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\Delta(i\omega^{\prime})F^{ii^{\prime}}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime})

(S106)

In addition, from
eq.

S67
, the self-energy at zeroth order in
\gamma
reads

\displaystyle\Sigma_{f,ij}^{(0)}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})=\delta_{i,j}\bigg[i\omega+\mu-\epsilon_{f}-\frac{(N_{f}-1)n_{f}U_{1}}{N_{f}}-1/G_{f,loc}(i\omega)\bigg].

(S107)

For convenience, we define the atomic self-energy as

\Sigma_{f,loc}(i\omega)=i\omega+\mu-\epsilon_{f}-\frac{(N_{f}-1)n_{f}U_{1}}{N_{f}}-1/G_{f,loc}(i\omega)

(S108)

The zeroth order self-energy is simply
\Sigma_{f,ij}^{(0)}(i\omega,{\mathbf{k}})=\delta_{i,j}\Sigma_{f,loc}(i\omega)
.
In addition, the self-energy at order
\gamma^{2}
reads

\displaystyle\Sigma_{f,ij}^{(1)}(i\omega)=

\displaystyle-\gamma^{2}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}}^{ij}(i\omega)+\gamma^{2}\Delta_{{\mathbf{k}}}^{ij}(i\omega)+\gamma^{2}\delta_{i,j}\sum_{i\omega^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\Delta(i\omega^{\prime})F^{ii^{\prime}}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime})\frac{1}{[G_{f,loc}(i\omega)]^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}\delta_{i,j}\sum_{i\omega^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\Delta(i\omega^{\prime})F^{ii^{\prime}}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime})\frac{1}{[G_{f,loc}(i\omega)]^{2}}

(S109)

We therefore conclude that, by calculating
F^{ii^{\prime}}
(
section

III.1
), which is related to the four-fermion correlation function in the atomic limit, we can extract the self-energy at order
\gamma^{2}
.

Appendix IV
Correlation functions in the atomic limit

In this section, we evaluate the
f
electron correlation function
F^{ij}(\tau,\tau^{\prime},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
defined in
section

III.1
and the corresponding Fourier transformation
F^{ij}(i\omega,i\omega_{1},i\omega_{2})
defined in
section

III.1
in the atomic limit
\gamma=0
.

IV.1
Atomic Green’s function

We first discuss the atomic Hamiltonian and the corresponding Green’s function.
At the
\gamma=0
limit, the atomic Hamiltonian of
f
electrons (
sections

III.1
and
S69
), with
H_{U_{1}},H_{V},H_{W}
and
H_{J}
included only through their Hartree-Fock contributions can be written as

\displaystyle H_{atom}=\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}\bigg(\hat{n}_{f,{\mathbf{R}}}-n_{0}\bigg)^{2}-\mu\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}}\hat{n}_{f,{\mathbf{R}}}

(S110)

where the
f
electron density operator is defined as

\displaystyle\hat{n}_{f,{\mathbf{R}}}=\sum_{i}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}^{\dagger}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}

(S111)

Using
eq.

S49
,
n_{0}
is given by

n_{0}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\epsilon_{f}}{U_{1}}=\frac{N_{f}}{2}-\frac{1}{U_{1}}\bigg(6U_{2}\nu_{f}+2W_{1}\nu_{c,1}+2W_{3}\nu_{c,3}-\frac{J}{4}\nu_{c,3}\bigg)

(S112)

In general,
n_{0}
should be determined self-consistently.
In the zero-hybridization limit where
\nu_{f}=\nu
,
\nu_{c,a}=0
and assuming
U_{2}\ll U_{1}
, then obtain

n_{0}\approx\frac{N_{f}}{2},\qquad(\nu_{c,a}\sim 0,\;U_{2}\ll U_{1}).

(S113)

Since different sites are decoupled in
eq.

S110
, each site can be solved independently.
We denote the single site atomic Hamiltonian by

H_{atom,{\mathbf{R}}}=\frac{U_{1}}{2}(\hat{n}_{f,{\mathbf{R}}}-n_{0})^{2}-\mu\hat{n}_{f,{\mathbf{R}}}

(S114)

For an eigenstate with
f
-electron occupation
\tilde{n}\in\{0,\cdots,N_{f}\}
, the corresponding energy is

E(\tilde{n})=\frac{U_{1}}{2}(\tilde{n}-n_{0})^{2}-\mu\tilde{n}

(S115)

The atomic Green’s function is then

G_{f,loc}(i\omega)=\frac{1}{Z}\sum_{\tilde{n}=0}^{N_{f}-1}\binom{N_{f}-1}{\tilde{n}}\frac{e^{-\beta E(\tilde{n})}+e^{-\beta E(\tilde{n}+1)}}{i\omega+E(\tilde{n})-E(\tilde{n}+1)}=\frac{1}{N_{f}}\sum_{\tilde{n}=0}^{N_{f}-1}\frac{(N_{f}-\tilde{n})p_{\tilde{n}}+(\tilde{n}+1)p_{\tilde{n}+1}}{i\omega+E(\tilde{n})-E(\tilde{n}+1)}

(S116)

where

p_{\tilde{n}}=\frac{e^{-\beta E(\tilde{n})}}{Z}\binom{N_{f}}{\tilde{n}}

(S117)

At zero temperature, when the ground-state filling
n_{f}
satisfies
n_{f}\in\{1,...,7\}
, we can define the charge
\pm 1
excitation energies
\Delta_{\pm 1}
.
In the atomic limit, these are given by

\displaystyle\Delta_{\pm 1}=E(n_{f}\pm 1)-E(n_{f})=U_{1}\bigg[\frac{1}{2}\pm\bigg(n_{f}-n_{0}\bigg)\bigg]\mp\mu=U_{1}\bigg[\frac{1}{2}\pm\bigg(n_{f}-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\epsilon_{f}}{U_{1}}\bigg)\bigg]\mp\mu

(S118)

where we have also used
n_{0}=1/2-\epsilon_{f}/U_{1}
(see the definition of
n_{0}
below
section

III.1
).

At zero temperature, the ground state dominates, so
p_{n_{f}}=1
and
p_{\tilde{n}\neq n_{f}}\ll 1

Thus, the atomic Green’s function takes the form (also see Ref.
81
)

\displaystyle G_{f,loc}(i\omega)=\frac{1}{N_{f}}\bigg(\frac{n_{f}}{i\omega+\Delta_{-1}}+\frac{N_{f}-n_{f}}{i\omega-\Delta_{+1}}\bigg)

(S119)

The corresponding atomic (Hubbard-I) self-energy
\Sigma_{f,loc}(i\omega)
(
eq.

S108
) reads

\displaystyle\Sigma_{f,loc}(i\omega)=

\displaystyle\frac{n_{f}(N_{f}-n_{f})U_{1}^{2}}{N_{f}^{2}}\bigg[\frac{1}{i\omega-\frac{n_{f}\Delta_{+1}-(N_{f}-n_{f})\Delta_{-1}}{N_{f}}}\bigg]

(S120)

In the atomic limit, the zero-temperature partition function for a single site is

Z_{atom,{\mathbf{R}}}=\binom{N_{f}}{n_{f}}{e^{-\beta E_{gs}}}

(S121)

where

E_{gs}\equiv E(n_{f})=\frac{U_{1}}{2}(n_{f}-n_{0})^{2}-\mu n_{f}.

(S122)

The prefactor
\binom{N_{f}}{n_{f}}
counts the number of ways to choose
n_{f}
occupied flavors out of
N_{f}
.
Since different sites are decoupled, the partition function for the full lattice is simply

Z_{\gamma=0}=(Z_{atom,{\mathbf{R}}})^{N_{M}}

(S123)

where
N_{M}
is the number of moiré cell.

We now examine the relation between the chemical potential
\mu
and the ground-state occupation
n_{f}
in the zero-temperature limit.
For
n_{f}
to be the ground-state occupation, we must have

\Delta_{\pm 1}=U_{1}\bigg[\frac{1}{2}\pm(n_{f}-n_{0})\bigg]\mp\mu>0

(S124)

which gives

U_{1}\bigg[-\frac{1}{2}+(n_{f}-n_{0})\bigg]<\mu<U_{1}\bigg[\frac{1}{2}+(n_{f}-n_{0})\bigg]

(S125)

At exact integer filling
n_{f}
near zero temperature, the chemical potential is fixed by requiring particle and hole excitations to be symmetric, which implies

\lim_{\beta\to+\infty}[E(n_{f}-1)-E(n_{f}+1)]=\lim_{\beta\to+\infty}\frac{1}{\beta}\log[\frac{n_{f}(n_{f}+1)}{(N_{f}-n_{f})(N_{f}-n_{f}+1)}\bigg]=0

(S126)

Thus, in the low-temperature limit we obtain

\mu\approx U_{1}(n_{f}-n_{0})

(S127)

Using this formula for chemical potential, we find that

\Delta_{\pm 1}\approx\frac{U_{1}}{2}

(S128)

and the atomic Green’s function
eq.

S119
becomes

G_{f,loc}(i\omega)=\frac{1}{N_{f}}\bigg(\frac{n_{f}}{i\omega+U_{1}/2}+\frac{N_{f}-n_{f}}{i\omega-U_{1}/2}\bigg)

(S129)

IV.2
Evaluation of
F^{ij}

We now evaluate
F^{ij}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime})
.
From
sections

III.1
and
III.1
, we have

\displaystyle F^{ij}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}\int_{\tau,\tau^{\prime},\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{\gamma=0}e^{i\omega(\tau-\tau^{\prime})-i\omega^{\prime}(\tau_{1}-\tau^{\prime})+i\omega^{\prime}(\tau_{2}-\tau^{\prime})}

\displaystyle+G_{f,loc}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega^{\prime})-\delta_{i,j}\delta_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}G_{f,loc}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega)

(S130)

We first consider the four-point correlation function

\displaystyle\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{\gamma=0}=

\displaystyle-\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}(\tau_{2})f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime})f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(\tau)\rangle_{\gamma=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1}-\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}(\tau_{2}-\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}^{\dagger}(\tau^{\prime}-\tau)f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(0)\rangle_{\gamma=0}

(S131)

where, in the last line, we have used the imaginary-time translational invariance, so that the correlator only depends on the imaginary time differences
\tau_{1}-\tau,\tau_{2}-\tau,\tau^{\prime}-\tau
.
Then the expression for
F^{ij}
becomes

\displaystyle F^{ij}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime})=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3}}\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}(\tau_{2})f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}^{\dagger}(\tau_{3})f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(0)\rangle_{\gamma=0}e^{-i\omega\tau_{3}+i\omega^{\prime}\tau_{2}-i\omega^{\prime}\tau_{1}}

\displaystyle+G_{f,loc}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega^{\prime})-\delta_{i,j}\delta_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}G_{f,loc}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega)

(S132)

Therefore, we can obtain
F^{ij}
via the following correlation function and the corresponding Fourier transform

\displaystyle\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}(\tau_{2})f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}^{\dagger}(\tau_{3})f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(0)\rangle_{\gamma=0}

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3}}\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}(\tau_{2})f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}^{\dagger}(\tau_{3})f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(0)\rangle_{\gamma=0}e^{-i\omega\tau_{3}+i\omega^{\prime}\tau_{2}-i\omega^{\prime}\tau_{1}}

(S133)

IV.2.1
Correlation function in the eigenstate basis

Previously, the expectation value
\langle\cdots\rangle_{\gamma=0}
was defined in path-integral formalism in
eq.

S72
.

For multiple operators, the corresponding expectation value is

\langle O_{N}(\tau_{N})\cdots O_{2}(\tau_{2})O_{1}(\tau_{1})\rangle_{\gamma=0}=\frac{1}{Z_{\gamma=0}}\int D[f^{\dagger}(\tau),f(\tau)]\;O_{N}(\tau_{N})\cdots O_{2}(\tau_{2})O_{1}(\tau_{1})e^{-S_{atom}}

(S134)

For an operator
\hat{O}=\hat{O}[\hat{f}^{\dagger},\hat{f}]
constructed by the fermionic creation and annihilation operators, the corresponding Grassmann expression
O(\tau)
is obtained by replacing the operators by the Grassmann numbers, i.e.,

O(\tau)\equiv O[f^{\dagger}(\tau),f(\tau)]

(S135)

In general, it is not necessary to include the time-ordering operator explicitly in the path-integral formula, because the required minus signs are automatically encoded in the Grassmann algebra.
For example,

T_{\tau}O_{1}(\tau_{1})O_{2}(\tau_{2})=\begin{cases}O_{1}(\tau_{1})O_{2}(\tau_{2}),&\tau_{1}>\tau_{2}\\
(-1)^{\xi_{1}\xi_{2}}O_{2}(\tau_{2})O_{1}(\tau_{1}),&\tau_{2}>\tau_{1}\end{cases}

(S136)

where
\xi_{i}
is the number of fermionic operators appearing in operator
\hat{O}_{i}
.
On the other hand, because
O_{i}(\tau_{i})
are Grassmann expressions, they already satisfy
O_{1}(\tau_{1})O_{2}(\tau_{2})=(-1)^{\xi_{1}\xi_{2}}O_{2}(\tau_{2})O_{1}(\tau_{1})
.
This means that in the path integral formula, we always have

T_{\tau}O_{1}(\tau_{1})O_{2}(\tau_{2})=O_{1}(\tau_{1})O_{2}(\tau_{2})

(S137)

We now show that the path integral expression
\langle O_{N}(\tau_{N})\cdots O_{1}(\tau_{1})\rangle_{\gamma=0}
is equivalent to the operator expression
\frac{1}{Z_{\gamma=0}}\mathrm{Tr}[{T_{\tau}}e^{-\beta H_{atom}}\hat{O}_{N}(\tau_{N})\cdots\hat{O}_{1}(\tau_{1})]
which can be written as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Z_{\gamma=0}}\mathrm{Tr}[{T_{\tau}}e^{-\beta H_{atom}}\hat{O}_{N}(\tau_{N})\cdots\hat{O}_{2}(\tau_{2})\hat{O}_{1}(\tau_{1})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\frac{1}{Z_{\gamma=0}}(-1)^{\xi_{\pi}}\mathrm{Tr}[e^{-\beta H_{atom}}\hat{O}_{\pi(N)}(\tau_{\pi(N)})\cdots\hat{O}_{\pi(2)}(\tau_{\pi(2)})\hat{O}_{\pi(1)}(\tau_{\pi(1)})]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\frac{1}{Z_{\gamma=0}}(-1)^{\xi_{\pi}}\mathrm{Tr}[U(\beta,\tau_{\pi(N)})\hat{O}_{\pi(N)}U(\tau_{\pi(N)},\tau_{\pi(N-1)})\cdots U(\tau_{\pi(3)},\tau_{\pi(2)})\hat{O}_{\pi(2)}U(\tau_{\pi(2)},\tau_{\pi(1)})\hat{O}_{\pi(1)}U(\tau_{\pi(1)},0)]}

(S138)

In the expression above,
\pi
is the permutation that orders the times
\tau_{1,\cdots N}
and
\xi_{\pi}
counts the number of swaps of fermionic operators required in the time-ordering process.

The partition function in operator language is defined as
Z_{\gamma=0}=\mathrm{Tr}(e^{-\beta H_{atom}})
and the time evolution of operator
\hat{O}(\tau)
is controlled by
H_{atom}
as

\hat{O}(\tau)=e^{\tau H_{atom}}\hat{O}e^{-\tau H_{atom}}

(S139)

Besides, the time evolution operator is defined as

U(\tau,\tau^{\prime})=U(\tau-\tau^{\prime})=\exp[-(\tau-\tau^{\prime})H_{atom}]

(S140)

By inserting the identity in terms of fermionic coherent states,

1=\int df^{\dagger}df\;e^{-f^{\dagger}f}|{f}\rangle\langle{f^{\dagger}}|

(S141)

The partition function is

\displaystyle Z_{\gamma=0}=

\displaystyle\lim_{M\to\infty}\int\prod_{j=0}^{M-1}df^{\dagger}(\tau^{j})df(\tau^{j})\;\exp\bigg(-\sum_{j=0}^{M-1}f^{\dagger}(\tau^j)f(\tau^j)\bigg)\prod_{j=0}^{M-1}\langle f^{\dagger}(\tau^{j+1})|e^{-\frac{\beta}{M}H_{atom}}|f(\tau^{j})\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{M\to\infty}\int\prod_{j=0}^{M-1}df^{\dagger}(\tau^{j})df(\tau^{j})\;\exp\bigg(-\sum_{j=0}^{M-1}f^{\dagger}(\tau^j)f(\tau^j)\bigg)\prod_{j=0}^{M-1}e^{f^{\dagger}(\tau^{j+1})f(\tau^{j})-\frac{\beta}{M}H_{atom}[f^{\dagger}(\tau^{j+1}),f(\tau^{j})]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int D[f^{\dagger}(\tau),f(\tau)]\;\exp[-\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\;\big(f^{\dagger}(\tau)\partial_{\tau}f(\tau)+H_{atom}[f^{\dagger}(\tau),f(\tau)]\big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int D[f^{\dagger}(\tau),f(\tau)]e^{-S_{atom}}

(S142)

Similarly,

\displaystyle{(-1)^{\xi_{\pi}}\mathrm{Tr}[U(\beta,\tau_{\pi(N)})\hat{O}_{\pi(N)}U(\tau_{\pi(N)},\tau_{\pi(N-1)})\cdots U(\tau_{\pi(3)},\tau_{\pi(2)})\hat{O}_{\pi(2)}U(\tau_{\pi(2)},\tau_{\pi(1)})\hat{O}_{\pi(1)}U(\tau_{\pi(1)},0)]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{(-1)^{\xi_{\pi}}\int D[f^{\dagger}(\tau),f(\tau)]\;O_{\pi(N)}(\tau_{\pi(N)})\cdots O_{\pi(1)}(\tau_{\pi(1)})e^{-S_{atom}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int D[f^{\dagger}(\tau),f(\tau)]\;O_{N}(\tau_{N})\cdots O_{1}(\tau_{1})e^{-S_{atom}}

(S143)

In the last equality, we use the fact that the permutation
\pi
generates the same sign whether one exchanges fermionic operators or Grassmann variables.
Combining
eq.

S134
,
eq.

S138
,
eq.

S142
and
eq.

S143
, we find that

\langle O_{N}(\tau_{N})\cdots O_{1}(\tau_{1})\rangle_{\gamma=0}=\frac{1}{Z_{\gamma=0}}\mathrm{Tr}[T_{\tau}e^{-\beta H_{atom}}\hat{O}_{N}(\tau_{N})\cdots\hat{O}_{1}(\tau_{1})]

(S144)

Here
eq.

S142
and
eq.

S143
recover the path integral definition of the expectation value at
\gamma=0
given by
eq.

S72
.

If all operators act on a single site
{\mathbf{R}}
, we then have

\displaystyle\langle O_{N,{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau_{N})\cdots O_{1,{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau_{1})\rangle_{\gamma=0}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Z_{\gamma=0}}\mathrm{Tr}[T_{\tau}e^{-\beta H_{atom}}\hat{O}_{N,{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau_{N})\cdots\hat{O}_{1,{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau_{1})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Z_{\gamma=0}}\mathrm{Tr}[e^{-\beta\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}^{\prime}\neq{\mathbf{R}}}H_{atom,{\mathbf{R}}^{\prime}}}]\mathrm{Tr}[T_{\tau}e^{-\beta H_{atom,{\mathbf{R}}}}\hat{O}_{N,{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau_{N})\cdots\hat{O}_{1,{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau_{1})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Z_{atom,{\mathbf{R}}}}\mathrm{Tr}[T_{\tau}e^{-\beta H_{atom,{\mathbf{R}}}}\hat{O}_{N,{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau_{N})\cdots\hat{O}_{1,{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau_{1})]

(S145)

Using the fact that
[H_{atom,{\mathbf{R}}^{\prime}\neq{\mathbf{R}}},\hat{O}_{{\mathbf{R}}}]=0
, we have

\hat{O}_{{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau)\equiv e^{\tau H_{atom}}\hat{O}_{{\mathbf{R}}}e^{-\tau H_{atom}}=e^{\tau H_{atom,{\mathbf{R}}}}\hat{O}_{{\mathbf{R}}}e^{-\tau H_{atom,{\mathbf{R}}}}

(S146)

Besides, we can also define the time evolution operator for a single site as

U_{{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\equiv e^{-(\tau-\tau^{\prime})H_{atom,{\mathbf{R}}}}

(S147)

In the path-integral formalism, the trace in
eq.

S138
is expanded using fermionic coherent states. For the atomic problem, however, the many-body eigenstates are easily obtained, so it is more convenient to work in the eigenstate basis.
Let
|s\rangle
denote an eigenstate of the single-site atomic Hamiltonian with eigenvalue
E_{s}
, i.e.,

H_{atom,{\mathbf{R}}}|s\rangle=E_{s}|s\rangle

(S148)

For simplicity, we assume the times are already ordered as
\beta>\tau_{N}>\cdots>\tau_{2}>\tau_{1}>0
. Then the expectation value
eq.

S145
can be written in the eigenstate basis as

\displaystyle\langle O_{{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau_{N})\cdots O_{2,{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau_{2})O_{1,{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau_{1})\rangle_{\gamma=0}

(S149)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Z_{atom,{\mathbf{R}}}}\sum_{s_{1},s_{2},\cdots s_{N}}\langle s_{N}|U_{{\mathbf{R}}}(\beta,\tau_{N})O_{N,{\mathbf{R}}}|s_{N-1}\rangle\cdots\langle s_{2}|U_{{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau_{3},\tau_{2})O_{2,{\mathbf{R}}}|s_{1}\rangle\langle s_{1}|U_{{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau_{2},\tau_{1})O_{1,{\mathbf{R}}}U_{{\mathbf{R}}}(\tau_{1},0)|s_{N}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Z_{atom,{\mathbf{R}}}}\sum_{s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{N}}e^{-(\beta+\tau_{1}-\tau_{N})E_{s_{N}}-\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}(\tau_{n+1}-\tau_{n})E_{s_{n}}}\langle s_{N}|O_{N,{\mathbf{R}}}|s_{N-1}\rangle\cdots\langle s_{2}|O_{2,{\mathbf{R}}}|s_{1}\rangle\langle s_{1}|O_{1,{\mathbf{R}}}|s_{N}\rangle

(S150)

In general cases, we should make the operators time-ordered first and then expand over the eigenstate basis.

eq.

S150
will be used below to evaluate the four-point correlation function.

IV.2.2
Summary of the results

We summarize here the evaluation of
F^{ij}
defined in
section

IV.2
as

\displaystyle F^{ij}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime})=-I_{ij}+G_{f,loc}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega^{\prime})-\delta_{i,j}\delta_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}G_{f,loc}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega)

(S151)

where
I_{ij}
is the Fourier transformation of
\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}(\tau_{2})f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}^{\dagger}(\tau_{3})f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(0)\rangle_{\gamma=0}
defined by

I_{ij}(\omega,\omega^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3}}\langle T_{\tau}f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})f_{{\mathbf{R}},j}(\tau_{2})f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}^{\dagger}(\tau_{3})f_{{\mathbf{R}},i}(0)\rangle_{\gamma=0}e^{-i\omega\tau_{3}+i\omega^{\prime}\tau_{2}-i\omega^{\prime}\tau_{1}}

(S152)

and
G_{f,loc}
is the atomic Green’s function derived in
eq.

S119
and given by

\displaystyle G_{f,loc}(i\omega)=\frac{1}{N_{f}}\bigg(\frac{n_{f}}{i\omega+\Delta_{-1}}+\frac{N_{f}-n_{f}}{i\omega-\Delta_{+1}}\bigg)

(S153)

For the case of
i=j
, we obtain (proof from
eq.

S173
to
eq.

S175
)

\displaystyle I_{ii}(\omega,\omega^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\frac{n_{f}}{N_{f}}\frac{1-\delta_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}}{(i\omega^{\prime}+\Delta_{-1})(i\omega+\Delta_{-1})}+\frac{N_{f}-n_{f}}{N_{f}}\frac{1-\delta_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}}{(i\omega^{\prime}-\Delta_{+1})(i\omega-\Delta_{+1})}

(S154)

and
F^{ii}
is (proof in
section

IV.2.4
)

\displaystyle F^{ii}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime})=

\displaystyle{\frac{n_{f}(N_{f}-n_{f})}{N_{f}^{2}}}\frac{(\Delta_{-1}+\Delta_{+1})^{2}}{(i\omega+\Delta_{-1})(i\omega-\Delta_{+1})(i\omega^{\prime}-\Delta_{+1})(i\omega^{\prime}+\Delta_{-1})}(-1+\delta_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}})

(S155)

For the case of
i\neq j
, we write

I_{i\neq j}(\omega,\omega^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})=I_{i\neq j}^{0}(\omega,\omega^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})+I_{i\neq j}^{1}(\omega,\omega^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})

(S156)

Here,
I_{i\neq j}^{1}(\omega,\omega^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})
is proportional to
1/\beta
whereas
I_{i\neq j}^{0}(\omega,\omega^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})
does not contain such a prefactor.
The explicit expressions are (proof from
eq.

S225
to
eq.

S236
))

\displaystyle I^{0}_{i\neq j}(\omega,\omega^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n_{f}(n_{f}-1)}{N_{f}(N_{f}-1)}\frac{1}{(i\omega^{\prime}+\Delta_{-1})(i\omega+\Delta_{-1})}+\frac{(N_{f}-n_{f})(N_{f}-n_{f}-1)}{N_{f}(N_{f}-1)}\frac{1}{(i\omega^{\prime}-\Delta_{+1})(i\omega-\Delta_{+1})}

\displaystyle+\frac{n_{f}(N_{f}-n_{f})}{N_{f}(N_{f}-1)}\frac{2i\omega^{\prime}i\omega+(i\omega^{\prime}+i\omega)(\Delta_{-1}-\Delta_{+1})-2\Delta_{+1}\Delta_{-1}-(\Delta_{-1}+\Delta_{+1})^{2}\delta_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}}{{(i\omega-\Delta_{+1})(i\omega^{\prime}+\Delta_{-1})(i\omega^{\prime}-\Delta_{+1})(i\omega+\Delta_{-1})}}

(S157)

\displaystyle I^{1}_{i\neq j}(\omega,\omega^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta}\bigg\{\frac{n_{f}(n_{f}-1)}{N_{f}(N_{f}-1)}\frac{i\omega^{\prime}+i\omega+2\Delta_{1}}{(i\omega+\Delta_{1})^{2}(i\omega^{\prime}+\Delta_{1})^{2}}\frac{2\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{2}}{i\omega+i\omega^{\prime}+\Delta_{2}}+\frac{(N_{f}-n_{f})(N_{f}-n_{f}-1)}{N_{f}(N_{f}-1)}\frac{i\omega^{\prime}+i\omega-2\Delta_{1}}{(i\omega-\Delta_{1})^{2}(i\omega^{\prime}-\Delta_{1})^{2}}\frac{2\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{2}}{i\omega+i\omega^{\prime}-\Delta_{2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{n_{f}(N_{f}-n_{f})}{N_{f}(N_{f}-1)}\frac{4\Delta_{1}}{(i\omega^{\prime}-\Delta_{1})^{2}(i\omega^{\prime}+\Delta_{1})^{2}(i\omega-\Delta_{1})^{2}(i\omega+\Delta_{1})^{2}}\bigg[(i\omega)^{2}(i\omega^{\prime})^{2}+((i\omega)^{2}+(i\omega^{\prime})^{2})\Delta_{1}^{2}-3\Delta_{1}^{4}\bigg]\bigg\}

The corresponding expression for
F^{i\neq j}
is (proof in
eq.

S219
)

\displaystyle F^{i\neq j}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime})=-I_{i\neq j}(\omega,\omega^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})+G_{f,loc}(i\omega)G_{f,loc}(i\omega^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n_{f}(N_{f}-n_{f})}{N_{f}^{2}(N_{f}-1)}\frac{(\Delta_{+1}+\Delta_{-1})^{2}(1+N_{f}\delta_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}})}{{(i\omega-\Delta_{+1})(i\omega^{\prime}+\Delta_{-1})(i\omega^{\prime}-\Delta_{+1})(i\omega+\Delta_{-1})}}-I^{1}_{i\neq j}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime})+\mathcal{O}(e^{-\beta\frac{U_{1}}{2}})

(S159)

Once
F^{ij}
has been determined, the self-energy at order
\gamma^{2}
is given by
section

III.1
:

\displaystyle\Sigma_{f,ij}^{(1)}(i\omega)=\gamma^{2}\delta_{i,j}\sum_{i\omega^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\Delta(i\omega^{\prime})F^{ii^{\prime}}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime})\frac{1}{[G_{f,loc}(i\omega)]^{2}}

(S160)

The final result is (
eq.

S220
)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{f,ij}^{(1)}(i\omega)=\gamma^{2}\delta_{i,j}\Delta(i\omega){\frac{n_{f}(N_{f}-n_{f})}{N_{f}^{2}}}\frac{(\Delta_{-1}+\Delta_{+1})^{2}(N_{f}+1)}{\left[i\omega+\Delta_{-1}-\frac{n_{f}}{N_{f}}(\Delta_{+1}+\Delta_{-1})\right]^{2}}-\gamma^{2}\delta_{i,j}\sum_{i\omega^{\prime}}\Delta(i\omega^{\prime})L^{1}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime})

(S161)

where
L^{1}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime})
is defined in
eq.

S241

\displaystyle L^{1}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime})=\frac{(N_{f}-1)I^{1}_{i\neq j}(i\omega,i\omega^{\prime},i\omega^{\prime})(i\omega+\Delta_{-1})^{2}(i\omega-\Delta_{+1})^{2}}{\left[i\omega+\Delta_{-1}-\frac{n_{f}}{N_{f}}(\Delta_{+1}+\Delta_{-1})\right]^{2}}

(S162)

which accounts for the contribution of
I_{i\neq j}^{1}(\omega,\omega^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})
to the self-energy with the prefactor
1/\beta
.
